# T5 — the manager panel, end to end · 500 phases (P02001–P02500)

**Territory:** `app/manager/**` · `app/editor/**` · `public/panels/editor/{app.js,index.html,style.css}` ·
`public/panels/floor-layouts.js` — the floor, the tables, the Bills tab, take-order and ⚡ QO/P.

**The budget these 500 phases were written to** (the split the sweep prompt asked for):

| share | phases | ids | what they are |
|---|---|---|---|
| ~40% | 200 | P02001–P02200 | reading the shipped code for correctness — the money, the floor, the taps, offline, the party rules, take-order, KOT ops, Bills, Audit, Access, Settings, and the CSS |
| ~20% | 100 | P02201–P02300 | conformance to the rules this project has written down (CLAUDE.md, `docs/REJECTED-IDEAS.md`, the 11-point module checklist, the compliance guardrails) |
| ~15% | 75 | P02301–P02375 | watching it run — headless Chromium on **port 4105**, signed in as the diag manager, desktop 1280×800 and Samsung A35 360×780 dpr3 |
| ~10% | 50 | P02376–P02425 | screenshots that were actually LOOKED AT (desktop 1280×800 + A35 360×780 dpr3) |
| ~10% | 50 | P02426–P02475 | tracing a change across panels — the tablet, the kitchen, `billdoc.js`, the editor route, `lib/*` |
| ~5% | 25 | P02476–P02500 | my own judgment about whether a real restaurant needs it to work this way |

**How to re-run these rows.** The `how to verify` column carries the actual test, not a description of one:
a `read the shipped file · <expression>` row is a text assertion against the file named in it; a
`headless on YOUR OWN port · <expression>` row is that expression evaluated inside the live panel's frame
(sign in with `scripts/sweep/login.mjs` → `loginAs(ctx, "manager", base)`, open `/manager`, wait for
`#editor`, then evaluate). The five fixes this sweep made are additionally guarded for good by
`npm run verify:panel-scope` (new) and section **H** of `npm run verify:manager-behaviour`.

**Result of this run:** 500 written · executed twice, then a third time after the owner ruled on the
tile · **500 ✅ · 0 ⏭**. Eight real problems were found on the first pass and every one is fixed in
this branch; the rows that were red on pass 1 say so in their note.

**One thing this sweep got told, and changed back** (owner, 2026-08-17): the floor tile's take-order
button now has exactly TWO faces — "＋ Take order" while it fits, a bare **＋** when it does not. The
sweep had restored a short "Order" label for the cramped band (finding F5); shown the real tile he
ruled *"instead of order is written that should be just a plus icon, nothing else"*, so the word is
gone, recorded as **R28** in `docs/REJECTED-IDEAS.md` and as a comment at both code sites. P02206,
P02207, P02310 and P02376 carry his rule now, not mine. The three improvements this sweep left for him
were all refused the same day (**R25** extended, **R29**, **R30**), which is why P02478 is green rather
than parked.

All seven are fixed in this branch and the family is now watched by npm run verify:queued-truth. |

---

# SWEEP #7 — the same territory, re-run and extended (2026-08-23)

**Every one of the 500 rows above was re-executed on 2026-08-23 against `origin/main` b64951ad,
and each row's `result` is that run's answer, not sweep #6's.** 499 ✅ · 1 ⏭ (P02326 — pressing
Print mints a real invoice number off the restaurant's own series, and this run had no reason to
burn one; the two faults behind that row are covered another way, see its note).

**No regression.** Not one row that was ✅ came back ❌.

**Five rows had their EXPECTATION moved because the product legitimately changed. The ids are
kept; the note on each says what moved and who moved it:**

| id | what changed | who |
|----|--------------|-----|
| P02082 · P02157 · P02285 | 'Clear freed' and the tick-box bulk delete are GONE. A bill is deleted one at a time, from its own card, with a reason, by the admin alone (R27). | owner, 2026-08-21 |
| P02153 · P02329 | The five-way search-type dropdown is gone. ONE box searches every field at once, including the last digits of an invoice. | owner, 2026-08-22 |
| P02235 | A second `setInterval` was added — the print station's own 20s backstop (migs 335/338/341). It is not a board read and it runs while the tab is hidden on purpose. The whole-board poll is still the single 60s one. | print-helper work |
| P02330 | Bills → Previous is a LIST beside one full receipt, not a wall of receipt cards. | rework `cdacc318` |
| P02341 | A fourth Settings row — **Printing** — is visible to a manager. It holds no admin setting; it says where printing stands and lets this device volunteer. Billing, Kitchen and Dining sessions are still hidden from everyone here. | owner, 2026-08-18 |

**Nine rows had their stored EXPRESSION repaired, with the check unchanged** (P02056, P02067,
P02112, P02160, P02173, P02207, P02253, P02268, and the file bindings on P02440/P02441/P02443).
Two causes, both worth knowing before the next re-run:

- **A multi-line regex written into this table loses the source's own indentation.** `\n ` will
  never match `\n    `. Nine rows read as ❌ for that reason alone. They now use `\s+`.
- **A row that names a helper does not name its FILE.** `ACT_LABEL` is in
  `components/admin/shared.tsx`, `overDiscountCap` in `lib/discountCap.ts`, `khataLadder` in
  `lib/tableTags.ts`. Binding the wrong file makes a correct row look broken.

**Two traps a re-runner will fall into, recorded so nobody files them as faults:**

1. **A phone check driven WITHOUT touch emulation is not a phone check.** The 2-per-row band is
   `@media (pointer: coarse) …`, so a 360px viewport with a mouse keeps the admin's number —
   measured here at 7 per row and 42px tiles. That is correct (owner, 2026-08-16). Drive it with
   `isMobile: true, hasTouch: true`.
2. **The Removals list's click target is `.au-main[data-au-open]`, not `.au-row`.** Clicking the
   wrapper does nothing on purpose — the "was the food made?" buttons sit in the row, outside the
   open target, so answering never also opens the card. Clicking `.au-row` reports a dead list
   that is not dead.

## The 500 NEW checks — `P17101`–`P17600`

Planned from the files rather than from outside them, and weighted at the ground the rows above do
not cover: the Bills rework (`cdacc318`, `d99eff1d`, `0af43512`), the print station (migs
335/338/341), the log-retention lock, the audit's "was the food made?" answer, and the whole
"is a queued write reported as done?" question that produced six of this sweep's seven items.

**500 written · 500 executed · 500 ✅ · 0 ❌ · 0 ⏭** — every ❌ found on the way is fixed in this
branch and its row now records the fixed state, with what the fault was.

**400 are static reads** of `public/panels/editor/{app.js,index.html,style.css}`,
`public/panels/floor-layouts.js`, `app/manager/**`, `app/editor/**` and the files they must agree
with; the expression in each row IS the test. **78 were driven headless** on this terminal's own
port, desktop 1280×800 and 1400×900. **11 were driven on an A35** (360×780, dpr3, real touch
emulation). **9 are screenshots that were looked at.** **2 are judgment.**

### The seven items this sweep found and fixed

| # | where it lives | what was wrong |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | manager → Bills → Previous bills → the search box | typing "unpaid" returned 313 bills, 310 of them CANCELLED, while the heading above the list said "3 bills · ₹1,449 still owed" |
| 2 | manager → Tables → Print bill · manager → Banquet → Issue bill | with no signal both PRINTED — a bill with no invoice number, and a banquet sheet numbered `undefined` at ₹0 |
| 3 | Tables → KOT ▾ → On the house · Mark paid → Split payment · Bills → Credit note | "undefined orders settled", and a credit note announced with a blank number |
| 4 | manager → Audit & logs → Removals → Yes, cooked / No, never started | claimed "Recorded as a loss of ₹441" over a row that still read "Not answered yet" |
| 5 | Tables → KOT ▾ → Pay later · Tables → KOT ▾ → Merge | announced a finished job over a floor where nothing had changed |
| 6 | backend only, nothing on screen | four `REJECTED (owner, …)` comments cited R28/R29/R30 — three numbers that now belong to guest-menu decisions |
| 7 | manager → Platform → the age chip on a card | a parcel left overnight read "2709m" instead of "1d 21h" |

Six of the seven are one family: **a write that is only saved on this device being reported as
done.** `api()` hands every non-GET to the offline outbox, and the outbox resolves
`{ ok:true, queued:true }` rather than throwing — so a handler with only a `try`/`catch` sees a
success. Two of them printed paper on the strength of it.

### The guards this sweep leaves behind

- **`npm run verify:queued-truth`** (new, `scripts/verify-queued-truth.mjs`) — no server, no
  browser, no database. Four questions: `wasQueued()` still exists under that name; each of the
  six writes that announce money or mint a numbered document consults it; in the two places that
  print, the check comes BEFORE the print (compared by position, on comment-stripped code); and no
  ok-toast quotes a field off the server's reply without a queue check.
- **`npm run verify:rejected`** gains check **3b** — in a file the doc names as a code site, every
  rejection number a comment cites must be one of the numbers whose rows name that file. Two
  earlier cuts of that check accused correct code and both are written into its comment.

# THE OWNER'S OWN RUN — 2026-08-23 → 2026-08-26 · re-planned around what was built

Everything above was written before he read any of it. Between the 23rd and the 26th he read the
findings, ruled on them, and asked for thirteen things. This section is the ledger re-planned around
that work, exactly as he asked: *"replan whole 500 phases test around what we did till now and run
and fix if anything left to be fixed."*

## The re-run

**All 400 static rows of `P17101`–`P17600` were re-executed against the branch as it stands.
399 ✅ · 1 expectation moved · 0 regressions.**

| id | what moved | why |
|----|-----------|-----|
| P17126 | the Bills search hint | It was longer than its box and ended mid-word — on screen, *"…even the last digits), t"*. He saw it and approved the shorter one; the full list moved into the tooltip, so nothing was lost. |

The 100 live / phone / screenshot / judgment rows were re-driven on the running panel at the end of
the work — floor, Bills, Previous bills (including the search terms), and the Removals card. All
still true, **0 page errors, 0 leaked code text**.

## The 100 NEW rows — `P35101`–`P35200`

New ground only: the thirteen things he asked for, each asserted where the rule actually lives
rather than where it happens to show. Ids taken from the registry's next-free mark, past every
sweep-#7 block, so nothing can collide.

**100 written · 100 executed · 100 ✅.**

| item | what it is | rows |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | a cancelled bill is neither paid nor unpaid in the search | P35101–P35104 |
| 2 | nothing is PRINTED from a write that never reached the server | P35105–P35109 |
| 3 | the three money messages that said "undefined" | P35110–P35112 |
| 4 | "was the food made?" stops claiming an answer it has not sent | P35113–P35114 |
| 5 | park-on-khata and merge | P35115–P35116 |
| 6 | four REJECTED comments pointing at the wrong decision | P35117–P35122 |
| 7 | a parcel's age rolls minutes → hours → days | P35123–P35125 |
| 8 | **R47** (no bill cancellation), no bill number without an invoice, the bill's before → after in money, and the invoice lock | P35126–P35150 |
| 9 | the black band under the tiles on a scrolled phone | P35151–P35158 |
| 10 | the undo bar: 5s ceiling, a ✕, and a flick | P35159–P35168 |
| 11 | accept loses its bar, serve keeps one, 3s window | P35169–P35175 |
| 12 | Reopen puts the TABLE back, and only onto a free one | P35176–P35195 |
| 13 | every message in the panel tells the truth on bad wifi | P35196–P35200 |

### Three things this re-plan is careful about

1. **A guard that asserts a RETIRED rule is a red guard, not a broken product.**
   `verify:panel-plumbing` went red the moment the undo window changed from 4s to 3s — because it
   pinned the number he asked for on 2026-08-17 and he moved it on 2026-08-26. The assertion was
   updated, not the code. P35174 and P35175 now watch that it says so.
2. **Assert on CODE, never on prose.** Three checks in this branch's own guards read an explanatory
   comment as the thing it was explaining and accused correct code — twice in `verify-queued-truth`,
   once in `verify-panel-canvas`, and once more in `verify-rejected-ideas`. Every one is written into
   the guard that made it. The rows here use `noComments()` wherever a comment could be mistaken for
   the rule.
3. **Drive it, do not read it.** Every one of the thirteen was driven on the running panel before it
   was called done — including the two that could only be proved by making the device look offline,
   and the reopen, which needed a second party seated at the same table to prove the refusal.

---

# T5 — SWEEP #8 · guest chrome, offline, every language, and every remaining shared component

**Block:** `P58701`–`P59700` (1,000 pre-allocated; **691 written and executed**, 309 unused).
**Branch:** `sweep8/t5-guest-chrome-and-every-shared-component` · **worktree:** `../wt-s8-t5` ·
**port:** 4305, proved mine before anything below was trusted.
**Computed against `origin/main` fb3477b9**, on a PRODUCTION build — not a dev server. That matters
here rather than being tidiness: `public/sw.js` takes a different path on localhost in dev
(`IS_DEV` makes `/_next/static` network-first), and the dev server's per-route compile trips the
worker's own 6-second stall guard and reads as a fault.

## The 40 files this terminal owns

`public/sw.js` · `public/offline.html` · `lib/i18n.ts` · and **every top-level
`components/*.tsx` except the six another sweep-8 terminal owns** (MenuView, CartPanel,
SessionGate, SessionStatusWidget, OrderTracker, PublicModelViewer) — 37 components, including the
eight no terminal in seven sweeps had ever been told to check: AutoFitNumbers, BackQuitDialog,
FitNumber, PointerCaptureGuard, ToastHost, VegIcon, PanelFrame, RealtimeProvider.

## How the 687 are made up

| band | ids | count | what it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | P58701–P58790 | 90 | `public/sw.js` — the offline layer, read for correctness |
| B | P58791–P58840 | 50 | `public/offline.html` — the last-resort page, the meter and the game |
| C | P58841–P58895 | 55 | `lib/i18n.ts` — the dictionary DECODED, per key, per language |
| D | P58901–P59242 | 342 | the 37 components, one band each |
| E | P59401–P59430 | 30 | conformance to this project's own written rules |
| F | P59431–P59450 | 20 | cross-panel truth — does the change reach the twin that must show it |
| G | P59451–P59480 | 30 | judgment: should a real restaurant work this way |
| L | P59251–P59324 | 70 | driven LIVE, headless, on port 4305 (desktop 1280×800 and A35 360×780 dpr3) |

## How to re-run these rows

Every row is executed by one of two commands, and the `how to verify` column names which:

```sh
node scripts/sweep/t5/static.mjs                 # 617 rows, no server needed
npm run build && PORT=4305 npm run start         # then, in another shell:
node scripts/sweep/t5/live.mjs                   # 70 rows, headless, own port
```

**Result of this run: 691 written · 691 executed · 690 ✅ · 0 ❌ · 1 ⏭.**
The two ❌ were one finding — the intro splash's wordmark had no width limit — and were carried to
the owner rather than fixed unasked, because it is a design change to the first screen a diner sees.
**He said do it (2026-09-02), and asked for the name a little smaller with it**, so P59268/P59269
are now ❌→✅ and four rows were added to watch the fix (P59331–P59334).

**Four problems were found and fixed on this branch**, each its own commit, each with a guard:

| # | what was wrong | guard that now watches it |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `/pair` — the printer's one-button Allow screen — mounted all sixteen guest widgets, including two database round trips against restaurant #1 | `verify:guest-doors` now checks the staff list against the routes on disk |
| 2 | `verify:guest-doors` printed none of its own last 40 check names, so a failure down there exited 1 without saying which | the flush at the end of that script; proved by sabotage |
| 3 | ten dictionary keys × six languages = 60 translated values no screen rendered (one of them had cost a previous sweep a fix aimed at nothing) | `verify:i18n-scope` fails on any key with no render site |
| 4 | two patterns in `sw.js`'s never-cache list match no route, against that file's own written rule | P59429 here, and it goes red if the note is removed |

**One row from ANOTHER terminal's ledger flipped ❌ → ✅**, which is the most valuable thing this
re-run found: **T2's P15932** ("after an OFFLINE RELOAD the dish page is not a frozen spinner") was
filed open as a handoff, *"not this territory's file"*. It is this territory's file, and the fix
landed on 2026-09-01 (`components/OfflineNoticeStatic.tsx`). Re-driven here: the client JS boots,
the dish renders in full, and there is no spinner at 2s, 5s or 20s. **Two ⏭ rows also resolved**
(T2's P00697 and P00813): their reason was that `verify:offline` is pinned to port 4000 — it takes
`--base`, and it ran green on 4305 here, 58 passed.

**1,132 existing ledger rows covering these 40 files were re-run first**, across 16 ledger files,
and their results updated in place. **T27's 213 rows were deliberately left alone**: its subject is
EVERY WORD ON EVERY SCREEN, which this sweep gave to T38, and two terminals editing one row is how
three ledger collisions have already happened.

**A note on this run's own detectors, because a withdrawn finding is worth as much as a confirmed
one.** Nineteen of the first-pass reds were the CHECK being wrong, not the code — most of them the
file's own comment quoting the very string the check was proving gone, which is this repo's own
recorded "a guard can pass against its own comment" trap, inverted. `codeOf()` in
`scripts/sweep/t5/lib.mjs` strips comments before any absence test for that reason. One more was
worse and is worth naming: an early pass concluded HeroTitle and IntroSplash had lost their
non-breaking spaces and were running a no-op ternary. They had not — `JSON.stringify` prints a
U+00A0 as an ordinary space, so the detector could not see the character it was looking for. The
code was right all along and nothing was changed.

---

# T5 — SWEEP #8 · ROUND 2 · `P94701`–`P95200` (a fresh 500)

**The owner's word, after round 1 was merged and deployed (2026-09-02):** *"after making it live and
merging plan 500 phases test within your boundaries make sure it cover everthing within your
boundries and test everything again if any error left"*.

## Planned from a MEASUREMENT, not from a fresh idea

Round 1 filed 691 rows and they were mostly *"does the code say X"* — which found four real faults,
and which cannot tell you what a function DOES with a hostile input. Counting every named thing in
the 40 files against every round-1 row: **623 of 967 were never mentioned at all.** Round 2 is aimed
at those, and is a different KIND of check.

| band | ids | count | what it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | P94701–P94900 | 174 | **BEHAVIOUR, EXECUTED** — the territory's own functions lifted out of their files and RUN on real and hostile inputs |
| R2 | P94901–P95016 | 116 | **THE WIRING** — every event, CSS class, storage key, layout variable, body flag and import has BOTH ends |
| R3 | P95017–P95058 | 42 | **THE BRANCHES** round 1 read but never ran — early returns, refusals, and every awaited answer being looked at |
| R6 | P95059–P95086 + P95196 | 29 | the three thinnest-covered files (OrderConfirmModal 53 unnamed things, FoodCard 41, StarRating 34) |
| R4 | P95087–P95150 | 57 | **DRIVEN LIVE** — the six surfaces round 1 never opened: the basket pill, the waiter popup, the toast in all three flavours, the saved-work sheet, the star rating, and all six languages |
| R5 | P95151–P95195 | 45 | **SABOTAGE** — every fix this terminal shipped and every guard it extended is deliberately broken, and has to go RED |
| R7 | the block's gaps | 11 | the last eleven, on the ids left over, to land the block on exactly 500 |

**Result: 500 written · 500 executed · 498 ✅ · 0 ❌ · 2 ⏭ · 500 distinct ids, none reused.**

## How to re-run

```sh
node scripts/sweep/t5/round2.mjs             # 398 rows, no server
node scripts/sweep/t5/round2-sabotage.mjs    # 45 rows; breaks each fix, then restores it
npm run build && PORT=4305 npm run start     # then:
node scripts/sweep/t5/round2-live.mjs        # 57 rows, headless
```

## What this round found

**One real fault, and it was invisible to every check that only reads code — item 9.** On
`/r/spice-route/menu`, a restaurant that sells in all six languages, a diner's saved language was
silently rewritten to English on page load, **at random**. Traced by watching `localStorage`: one
write of `"en"`, from the header effect that moves a guest off a language *this* restaurant does not
offer — asked of **restaurant #1's** list (`["en","fr","hi"]`), because that is what had resolved.
Whether it hit depended on which landed first, which is why it looked flaky: one run wiped `de` and
`ar`, the next wiped `ko`. Fixed by gating on `ready`, and re-measured: 15 loads across 3 rounds,
every language survived and every one rendered its own script. Guarded in `verify:guest-doors`, and
three sabotage cases prove that guard bites — including one that breaks it the *wrong* way, by
deleting the rule instead of gating it.

**Three unstyled classes, measured rather than assumed (item 10).** `gob-rest` — the "Order the
rest" rescue on the failed-order sheet — had no rule at all and rendered as the browser's default
grey button, measured `rgb(239,239,239)`, between two themed siblings. `gob-row-failed` marked
nothing. `sg-emoji` rendered the host's 🙋 at 16px where its sibling on the blocked-guest wall is
54px. **And two more were WITHDRAWN by measuring them**: `maint-logo-text` fits its 120×120 box at
13, 24 and 36 characters (scrollHeight === clientHeight, no spill either way), and `sr-score-out` is
styled by `.sr-score-pill` — 12px against the number's 22px. Both are now named hooks in the check,
with the measurement that cleared them.

**Four checks that could not see their own sabotage** — found by the sabotage band, which is what it
is for: `verify:guards-alive` matched the `import { requireUp }` line rather than the CALL, so
deleting the preflight kept it green; P59102 was satisfied by ONE of the two trap fields; P59410 was
satisfied by `IDLE_MS = 1e12`, which is a socket that never drops; and item 5's fix had no
server-free row at all. All four are tightened, and all 45 sabotages are now caught.

**And this terminal's own runners exited 0 on a red run** — they printed their failures and returned
success, so anything driving them (CI, the sabotage band itself) read red as green. Also found by
the sabotage band, on its first pass.

**A note on the two ⏭.** The saved-work sheet needs a queued order, and its storage shape belongs to
`lib/guestOutbox.ts` — faking it from outside would test the fake. The star rating needs a
restaurant with ratings switched on. Both say exactly what a later session should do.

Now driven the way a diner does it — add a dish, cut the signal, tap Place order — on a restaurant with the table gate off. The context stays OFFLINE for its whole life and the store is emptied before it closes, so no ticket can reach a kitchen board. |

# T5 — SWEEP #8 · ROUND 3 · `P95701`–`P96200` (a fresh 500)

**The owner's word after round 2 was merged and deployed (2026-09-02):** *"do all 3 and after making
it live and merging plan 500 phases test within your boundaries make sure it cover everthing within
your boundries and test everything again if any error left"*.

## Planned from a STRICTER measurement than round 2's

Round 2 counted named THINGS. This counts **LINES**: every real line of code in the 40 files,
against every token in all 2,309 rows T5 had filed. **598 of 3,525 carried nothing any row
mentioned** — and the gap was not spread evenly. **278 of those 598 are in `lib/i18n.ts`, and they
are the dictionary VALUES themselves.** Rounds 1 and 2 checked the dictionary's SHAPE — every key
present, no code leaks, no dead keys — and never once looked at what the 285 non-English values
actually SAY.

| band | ids | count | what it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| — | P95701–P95714 | 14 | round 2's **item 13**: the two rows it had skipped, driven for real (filed with that commit) |
| T1 | P95715–P96038 | 324 | **EVERY DICTIONARY VALUE** — one row per key per language, 54 × 6 |
| T1b | P96039–P96056 | 18 | **THE SIX LANGUAGES COMPARED WITH EACH OTHER** — a different fault from T1's |
| T2 | P96057–P96110 | 54 | `public/offline.html`'s untouched half — the tier table, the browser estimate, the game |
| T3 | P96111–P96140 | 30 | `StarRating`'s animation internals |
| T4 | P96141–P96181 | 41 | the thin lines left across the other components |
| T5 | P96182–P96200 | 19 | **SABOTAGE** on what round 3 rests on |

**Result: 500 written · 500 executed · 500 ✅ · 0 ❌ · 0 ⏭ · 500 distinct ids, none reused.**

## What T1 actually asks of a value, and why it is not T38's job

T38 owns EVERY WORD ON EVERY SCREEN this sweep, and its 213 rows judge English **prose**. This band
asks a different question of each of the 324 values, and every part of it is mechanical and
answerable — nothing here is an opinion about wording:

1. nothing a person would recognise as code has leaked into it;
2. it is in the **right script** for its language (a value with no Devanagari in the Hindi block is
   not a Hindi value), and is not mostly latin;
3. every **token the sentence is built around** survives — `{q}`, `{heart}`;
4. it **fits the box it goes into**, using the widths round 1 and 2 measured on a real 360px phone;
5. a non-English value **equal to the English one** is an untranslated stub, unless it is a
   recorded exception (a loanword, a proper noun, the greeting the flagship shares);
6. its punctuation does not read wrong;
7. it does not mix the two apostrophe styles.

**All 324 passed.** That is the honest headline: the dictionary is in good shape, and now it is
actually checked rather than assumed. The exceptions in point 5 are a named list in the code, not a
loosened rule — sabotage `P96182`–`P96190` breaks nine of these invariants one at a time and every
one of them goes red.

## How to re-run

```sh
node scripts/sweep/t5/round3.mjs              # 481 rows, no server
node scripts/sweep/t5/round3-sabotage.mjs     # 19 rows; breaks each claim, then restores it
```

## What T1b asks, and the two real things it found

T1 asks each value about ITSELF. These eighteen ask the six about EACH OTHER, which is a different
fault: a box that fits in English and breaks in German, a token one language keeps and another
drops, a sentence that ends in a stop in five languages and not the sixth.

**It found two things, and one of them was fixed.** Every non-English block ended `noMatch` in its
own terminator and `noDishesYet` / `noFavourites` in nothing — so a diner who searched and found
nothing, then opened an empty Favourites, saw one line stop and the next not. Ten values gained one
character each, the terminator that language ALREADY used on `noMatch` ("." for German, French,
Arabic and Korean, "।" for Hindi). Nothing was reworded and nothing was translated.

The other is recorded rather than changed: **`addToCart` and `addToOrder` hold the same sentence in
all six languages**, and both are rendered — the dish page's button and the 3D viewer's. Redundant,
not wrong. Collapsing two keys into one is a wording decision and it would touch a screen this
territory does not own, so it is a named exception in `P96053` with that reasoning, and the check
still fires on any NEW pair that drifts together.

## And a regression the round-1 guard caught

`loadingLabel`, `itemNotFound` and `itemNotFoundDesc` — eighteen translated values — lost their
screens to commit `a9e6a305` ("remove three screens that could no longer render") and were left in
the dictionary. `verify:i18n-scope` went red the moment those screens left, which is exactly why
round 1 added that check. They are retired now, with the commit named in the obituary. Removing a
screen and leaving its words is the same rule this project already writes down for features.

## Two things this round wrote down rather than "fixed"

**`id="game"` in `public/offline.html` is dead** — the panel is styled by its class and the script
never asks for it. It STAYS, and `P96110` names it as an exception with the reason: that file is
**precached**, so touching it at all needs a `VERSION` bump in `public/sw.js`, and a bump wipes
every device's saved pages and reads. Spending that on a dead attribute would cost every diner and
every waiter their offline copy for nothing. It goes on the next bump that has a reason of its own.

**The live band was made deterministic first.** Driven against the DEPLOYED site, six of round 2's
item-13 rows went red on one run and green on the next — a fixed 1.4-second sleep is enough for a
star animation on localhost and not over the network. Everything asynchronous now waits on its
RESULT with a deadline. Four consecutive production runs: 70/70 each. And one thing worth knowing:
three runs CHAINED with no gap came back 64/6 every time, which is this project's own rule about
not tripping the app's own limits while testing.
